@@132553598 <h> Sugar cane farmers in Kenya set to benefit from Chinese seed variety <p> A Chinese investor in Kenya is on a mission to improve sugarcane production and offer Kenyan farmers better incomes for their produce . <p> The investor , Jack Liu , who is working with a team of researchers through a Kenyan-based <p> firm Crevation International Limited is currently developing two new tissue culture sugarcane seed varieties , focusing on early maturation , drought and disease resistance as well as high sugar content to boost farmer income and mitigate climate change effects on agriculture . <p> Liu and his team of 15 experts have already received approval from the Kenya Plant Health Inspectorate Services ( Kephis ) to conduct trials in Rongai Sub-County on the ' Golden Fruit 2 variety ' which is being developed for the manufacture of industrial sugar and ' Golden Fruit 1 variety ' meant for chewing or manufacture of sugarcane juice . <p> In May 2022 , Liu leased 50 acres of land in Kambi Ya Moto Area in Rongai Constituency of Nakuru County , where he started conducting the trials . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ site where companies , organisations , and institutions are coming to learn about the two sugarcane varieties , which are set to be released to the market in June 2026 subject to approval by Kephis . <p> Liu indicates that the improved seed varieties whose technology originates from China mature in 7-10 months , compared to most varieties in the local market that take 18-24-month cycles , making farming more profitable and sustainable by reducing crop loss and increasing yields . <p> The agripreneur states that new varieties also offer increased sucrose content and overall yield , enhancing profitability for farmers in key sugar-growing regions . <p> Mr Liu indicates that the two varieties once approved by Kephis will be distributed to farmers in Western Kenya ( Kakamega , Bungoma , Busia , Kisumu , Migori , Nandi ) , parts of the Rift Valley ( Narok , Trans-Nzoia , and Kericho ) , and parts of coastal region ( Kwale , Tana River , Kilifi , Lamu ) . <p> He states that trials have established that the ' Golden Fruit 2 Variety ' has 80 percent juice content @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ get 15 tons of refined sugar from 100 tons of sugarcane as it has at least 50 percent more sucrose than traditional seed varieties . <p> Liu assured farmers that the new sugarcane breeds are designed to withstand climate change shocks , offering better drought tolerance and pest/disease resistance . <p> " Sugarcane is a water-intensive crop . It thrives best in regions with consistent rainfall of 1,100 -- 2,500 mm annually , warm temperatures and well-drained , fertile soils . Counties like Kakamega , Bungoma , Busia , parts of Nyanza and the Rift Valley have long provided the ideal environment for cane farming , " states the agri-preneur . <p> " Today , farmers in these areas will tell you that the rains no longer come as they used to . Sometimes , droughts are prolonged ; when the skies finally open , heavy downpours result in flooding . It is harder than ever to predict the seasons -- and that is why the sugar sector needs resilient seed varieties , " he adds . <p> Mr Liu stated that Crevation International Limited was determined to contribute to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ that are more resilient , productive and economically viable for farmers . <p> " Our efforts aim to overcome challenges with disease-prone varieties , making sugarcane farming more profitable by increasing yield per acre and reducing time to harvest . It is our sincere hope that the development will encourage farmers to double their efforts amid challenges bedeviling the sub-sector , " he added . <p> He noted that quality sugarcane seed is crucial for Kenyan farmers as it produces healthier , more robust plants , directly leading to more cane and better income . <p> Liu said that lack of certified seed often constraints farmers to use poor-quality material from ratoon crops ( second-generation growth ) or cane tops , leading to poor establishment and low yields . <p> Presently , the average sugarcane yield in Kenya is around 60.52 tonnes per hectare , far below its capacity of between 90-150 tonnes per hectare . <p> Liu observes that since sugarcane is propagated vegetatively , it favours the accumulation of pathogens . This , he elaborates , is because the disease-causing pathogens are introduced into the new areas along @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ different pathogens over time makes minor diseases become major ones . Disease-infected seed cane can play a significant role in the multiplication and spread of diseases , as has been the case with red rot , smut , wilt , grassy shoot , ratoon stunting , yellow leaf and leaf scald . Unfortunately , we have operated the industry without following the scientific system of seed cane production , " he lamented . <p> According to Liu , a robust seed cane system is essential to reversing this trend . He states that the ideal structure comprises three critical and interdependent stages : breeder seed , foundation seed , and certified seed . <p> Breeder seed is the first tier in the seed cane pipeline . It is preserved by research institutions under strictly controlled environments to maintain genetic integrity . Derived from nucleus stock , breeder seed is grown in isolation and subjected to rigorous monitoring to prevent contamination . <p> Foundation seed , he explains , serves as the intermediary stage between breeder and certified seed . It is cultivated under stringent agronomic and phytosanitary protocols , typically @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ multipliers . The goal is to produce sufficient volumes of clean , high quality planting material to supply certified seed producers while safeguarding varietal purity and health standards . <p> Certified seed , the third and most critical tier , Mr Liu elaborates , is what farmers directly plant in their fields . <p> With regard to seed propagation , the agri-preneur explains that one acre can hold up to 6000 seedlings of each variety . <p> " Each seedling can propagate 20 to 30 stems but we recommend that each seedling propagates a maximum of 5 to 10 seedlings . This means any farmer propagating either of the two varieties on an acre will yield at least 30,000 stems , " he notes . <p> Crevation International Limited Marketing Manager , Penina Karimi , notes that with proper land preparation , choice of the right varieties , and good market planning , sugarcane cultivation in Kenya can provide steady income and attractive returns . <p> The main sugarcane-growing areas in Kenya include Western Kenya ( Kakamega , Bungoma , Busia ) , Nyanza ( Kisumu , Migori ) , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> She notes that focus on advancing research , innovation and technology transfer within the Kenyan sugar industry must take the lead in breeding drought-tolerant , pest-resistant cane varieties that mature faster and require less water . <p> Furthermore , Ms Karimi points out that partnerships between national and county governments should focus on proactive extension services , including training on soil conservation , mulching and intercropping to build resilience at the farm level . <p> " Climate change is not a distant threat -- it is already affecting cane in the fields and the lives it supports , from farmers to mill workers , rural communities and the national economy . This new reality is rewriting the rules of agriculture in real time . We can not assume that sugarcane will continue to thrive as it always has , " observes the Marketing Manager . <p> According to Liu , the grass , which is locally called " Chinese magic grass " , can increase milk production by 50 per cent thanks to its high protein content and reduce the dairy costs at processing and packaging as silage @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ said , has 18.6 percent crude protein content , taking the second position after Lucerne , which has 22 per cent , Napier at 10.8 , maize leaves at 8 per cent and Boma Rhodes Hay at 2 per cent .

" This shift goes hand-in-hand with climate change pressures , he adds . @@132682911 <h> Funny <h> Japan 's Aomori apple growers turn to peaches amid rising temperatures <p> Apple growers in Aomori Prefecture 's Tsugaru region are increasingly struggling with the effects of climate change . Early-season apple varieties , harvested from September , are becoming less suited to rising temperatures , which can damage the fruit , causing discoloration or sunburn on the skin . <p> Hirakawa farmer Ono , 47 , said heat damage became so burdensome that he replaced some apple trees with peaches . These " Tsugaru no momo " peaches , with sugar content above 11% , are less affected by heat and fetch high prices , offering a more reliable livelihood . Conversion to peach orchards began about 20 years ago , partly to allow harvests before the typhoon season . Rising temperatures have accelerated the trend , according to the Tsugaru Mirai Agricultural Cooperative Association . <p> Peach cultivation in the Tsugaru region has grown from 48 hectares across seven municipalities in 2015 to 73 hectares in 2023 , with over 200 producers currently involved . <p> The Agriculture , Forestry and Fisheries Ministry is supporting @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ and installing shading nets . @@132792466 <h> Thank you for reading Nation.Africa <p> Thirty years after the Beijing Declaration , many Kenyan girls are still being told that technology is for boys , that coding is a hobby for geniuses in hoodies , and that leadership in the digital world is a male preserve . <p> Yet every day , the same girls are navigating complex online spaces , running side hustles on their phones , and helping their families access services through digital tools . The contradiction is glaring . At a moment when technology is reshaping everything from banking to farming , leaving women on the margins is not just unfair . It is a direct threat to Kenya 's future . <p> Globally , women remain seriously underrepresented in the technology workforce and almost invisible at the top . Less than one third of jobs in science , technology , engineering and mathematics are held by women , and their share of leadership roles is even smaller . In artificial intelligence and cyber security , women are still a small minority . Kenya mirrors this pattern . <h> Also Read <p> Women make @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ third of students in STEM courses , and barely more than a tenth in engineering . Fewer than three in ten ICT graduates are women . These are not just cold numbers . They tell the story of classrooms where girls sit quietly at the back , innovation hubs where they are present but seldom in charge , and boardrooms where decisions about our digital future are made without their voices . <h> Digital payments <p> The Beijing Declaration called for universal rights and equal opportunities . In the digital age , that vision means much more than letting girls own phones or open social media accounts . It means ensuring that women shape the very tools that are changing how we live , work and govern . <p> Technology that is not created with women in mind will always fall short for half the population . It will miss the realities of a farmer in Turkana using a mobile app to check weather patterns , a market trader in Kisumu using digital payments to keep her business afloat , or a young mother in Mandera consulting an online health @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ The first barrier is still basic access . The Women 's Empowerment Index in Kenya shows that girls and women in rural and frontier counties face severe deprivation in education and digital literacy . In places like Mandera , Turkana , Samburu and Wajir , the digital world can feel as distant as another planet . That gap is not just about cables and devices . It is about opportunity . When a girl can not learn how to use a computer , she is locked out of entire careers before she even finishes school . When a woman can not access reliable internet , she can not compete fairly in e commerce , agritech , or online services . <p> Yet there are powerful signs of what is possible when we intentionally open doors . Through the Women in Technology at Huawei Club and programmes like the DigiTruck , which takes digital literacy training to communities across Kenya , we have seen young women who had never touched a keyboard build the confidence to code , troubleshoot networks , and imagine themselves as innovators . <h> STEM clubs <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , we meet girls who are no longer content to be the only woman in the lab . They want to lead teams , start companies , and design solutions that respond to challenges they know intimately . In the 2025 Huawei ICT Competition , four young Kenyan women stood as finalists on a global stage , a glimpse of what our country can achieve when we invest in their talent . <p> Women have a multiplier effect . When a woman gains a digital skill , she does not keep it to herself . She trains her siblings , helps neighbours apply for jobs online , supports her parents to access government services , and shares information that can save lives . Women leaders in technology are already driving solutions to climate change , maternal health , urban safety and public accountability . <p> Imagine early warning systems for floods designed by women who have seen their homes washed away , mobile platforms for maternal health created by women who have survived risky pregnancies , or civic apps shaped by women who bear the brunt of broken services . @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , problems are framed differently and solved more completely . <p> Ignoring women 's leadership in technology does not save resources . It wastes them . It means training half our population only to confine many of them to low paid work while high value digital jobs go to a narrow group . <p> It means designing national platforms for health , education or climate adaptation without tapping into the everyday intelligence of women who hold households and communities together . And the pipeline does not fix itself . Without deliberate mentorship , scholarships , inclusive workplaces and policies that support work life balance , many talented women will continue to drop out before they reach leadership . <p> If Kenya is serious about becoming a digital powerhouse , we must treat women 's leadership in technology as a national priority . That means expanding digital literacy for girls in rural and marginalised areas , so that every girl can graduate with basic ICT skills . It means building strong mentorship networks that link girls to women already thriving in tech , finance , health and public service . <p> @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in a smarter , fairer and more competitive Kenya . When women lead in technology , countries leap forward . Kenya has the talent , the drive and the moral duty to prove it . <p> Follow our WhatsApp channel for breaking news updates and more stories like this .

@@132883886 <h> Counting the Cost 2025 : A year of climate breakdown <h> Attachments <p> New report identifies the ten most expensive and impactful climate disasters of the year . <p> Heatwaves , wildfires , droughts , and storms cost the world more than $120 billion in 2025 , according to a new Christian Aid report , Counting the Cost 2025 . The report underscores the escalating cost of climate change , with fossil fuel companies playing a central role in driving the crisis . The cost of climate inaction is equally clear , as communities continue to bear the brunt of a crisis that could have been averted with urgent action to reduce greenhouse gas emissions . <p> Key findings : <p> The report identifies the year 's 10 costliest extreme events influenced by the climate crisis , each causing over $1 billion in damage . Among these , the Palisades and Eaton wildfires in California alone cost more than $60 billion . <p> It examines 10 other significant extreme events that caused massive human and environmental damage , with the greatest toll felt in the world 's poorest counties @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ record-breaking heatwaves in Scotland . Other major events highlighted in the report include the extensive droughts in Canada , the series of typhoons in the Philippines , and record-breaking fires in the Iberian Peninsula . <p> Christian Aid calls for urgent action to cut emissions and ramp up support for vulnerable communities , stressing that the impacts of these disasters can be mitigated with the right steps . <p> " These disasters are not ' natural ' -- they are the predictable result of continued fossil fuel expansion and political delay , " said Emeritus Professor Joanna Haigh , at Imperial College London . <p> The ten most financially costly events all had an impact of more than $1 billion with the combined total topping more than $122 billion in damage . Most of these estimates are based only on insured losses , meaning the true financial costs are likely to be even higher , while the human costs are often uncounted . <p> The report also highlights ten extreme weather events that did n't rack up big enough insured losses to make the top ten but were just as @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in poorer countries where many people do n't have insurance and where data is less available . <p> In terms of events which caused the biggest financial cost in 2025 , the US bore the brunt , with the fires in California topping the list as the single biggest one-off event at $60 billion in damage and leading to the deaths of more than 400 people . Second on the list were the cyclones and floods that struck Southeast Asia in November causing $25 billion in damage and killing more than 1,750 people across Thailand , Indonesia , Sri Lanka , Viet Nam and Malaysia . Third were the devastating floods in China which displaced thousands , caused $11.7 billion in damage and killed at least 30 . <p> No continent was spared from crippling climate disasters in 2025 , with at least one disaster in each of the six populated regions of the world making the report . Drought in Brazil , February cyclones in Australia and R ? union island off the coast of Africa and summer wildfires in Spain and Portugal meant no corner of the world @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ top six costliest disasters with flooding in India and Pakistan killing more than 1,860 people , costing up to $6 billion and affecting more than 7 million people in Pakistan alone . More than $5billion in damage was caused by typhoons in the Philippines with more than 1.4 million people displaced . <p> While the top ten focuses on financial costs , which are usually higher in richer countries because they have higher property values and can afford insurance , some of the most devastating extreme weather events in 2025 hit poorer nations , which have contributed little to causing the climate crisis and have the least resources to respond . <p> These included flooding in Nigeria in May , and the Democratic Republic of Congo in April , which in combination affected thousands of people with up to 700 deaths in Nigeria alone . The ongoing drought in Iran and West Asia threatens the 10 million people in Tehran with possible evacuation due to a water crisis . <p> Making the second list of 10 was also some events that stood out for being particularly unusual such as the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Scotland , with 47,000 hectares burned . Japan faced an extreme year of both heavy snowstorms and record-breaking heatwaves . Worrying climate related extremes were also recorded in Antarctica and the world 's oceans which recorded record breaking sea temperatures and coral bleaching in West Australia which pose major threats to biodiversity . <p> Christian Aid says these extreme events highlight the need for more urgent action to reduce carbon emissions and accelerate the transition to renewable energy and underlines the importance of providing funding for vulnerable people . <p> Christian Aid CEO , Patrick Watt , said : <p> " This year has once again shown the stark reality of climate breakdown . Violent storms , devastating floods and prolonged droughts are turning lives and livelihoods upside down . The poorest communities are first and worst affected . <p> " These climate disasters are a warning of what lies ahead if we do not accelerate the transition away from fossil fuels . They also underline the urgent need for adaptation , particularly in the global South , where resources are stretched and people are especially vulnerable to climate shocks @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is a political choice . It is being driven by decisions to continue burning fossil fuels , to allow emissions to rise , and to break promises on climate finance . In 2026 , world leaders must act - supporting communities already adapting at a local level , and providing the resources urgently needed to protect lives , land and livelihoods . " <p> " The world is paying an ever-higher price for a crisis we already know how to solve . These disasters are not ' natural ' -- they are the inevitable result of continued fossil fuel expansion and political delay . While the costs run into the billions , the heaviest burden falls on communities with the least resources to recover . Unless governments act now to cut emissions and fund adaptation measures , this misery will only continue . " <p> " This year lays bare the brutal reality of climate change . While wealthy nations count the financial cost of disasters , millions of people across Africa , Asia and the Caribbean are counting lost lives , homes and futures . In 2026 , governments @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ responding with real support for the people on the frontlines -- through scaled-up finance for those in need and faster emissions reductions . " <p> " The events documented in this report are not isolated disasters or acts of nature . They are the predictable outcome of a warmer atmosphere and hotter oceans , driven by decades of fossil fuel emissions . Climate change is now measurably amplifying extreme weather across the world , increasing both its intensity and its cost in terms of lives lost , livelihoods destroyed , and economies damaged . What we are seeing in 2025 is not a warning of the future ; it is the present reality of climate breakdown . "
